2026-08-04 | Indentation *recovery* improved: a wrong margin is rounded to the nearest level, and a jump never opens more than one level (clamped), while both diagnostics fire unchanged. Measured on tests/golden/check/indentation.hero: 2 mistakes produced **5** diagnostics, now produce **2**. The distinction is stated in the code because it will look like a contradiction later: §4.15's "errors, never interpretations" governs what the compiler *accepts* — the program is still rejected — while recovery governs only the structure handed to the next stage, and an implausible structure arrives as extra diagnostics about a mistake already reported | author instruction, debrief 2026-08-04 ("layout → migliora"), chosen over gating the parser after a layout error: gating would have hidden genuine parse errors in the rest of the file, while better recovery hides nothing. M1's two ratified adversarial cases keep their inputs and their diagnostics; their snapshots and stated rationale are updated (the jump case used to praise honouring two levels — nothing in the language opens two) | §4.15 | — |
2026-08-04 | A `match` arm's body is one statement, inline, or an indented block (panel 014, adopted provisionally — author ratification queued). §4.7 also gains the two rules the judges converged on: a jump (`break`/`continue`/`return`) is admissible as an arm body and must NOT be typed `()` (RFC 1216's recorded wrong turn), and a `match` used as a *value* requires every arm to produce a value — a checker rule at M3c, orthogonal to the spelling. R (`assert` as an expression) VETOED by the compiler-engineer: no rejection site for `x = assert y`, a §4.14 precedence row to invent, and Part 5's desugaring has no value for a three-address temp; the spec-warden's cheaper S is R's parser half and dies with it. The `?`/M4 rationale put to the judges is STRUCK from the record as false | panel 014 — Q was recorded as "0 lines" and is not: the engineer built P and measured net +10 lines in 5 frontend files (0 in checker/ownership/emitter) while finding three shipped defects with one cause, the `ArmBody::Expr` bypass — `heroes fmt` deleting an arm's `if` branches, `heroes parse` never terminating, and a doubled diagnostic. The ergonomist showed the guard Q appeared to provide never existed (the block form re-admits every valueless arm). Kotlin's grammar is P verbatim; Scala's since 2.x. v1 spec text measured: bare +1, enumerated with the "`if` is an expression" duplicate absorbed +10 (2058), the value rule a further +18 and deferred to M3c | §4.7, §1.7 | 014
2026-08-04 | Resolver rejection set fixed (panel 015, adopted provisionally — author ratification queued). **A**: unused reaches parameters (`extern` exempt, top-level declarations not covered), `_` legal and repeatable as a parameter name, and §4.16's hole suppression covers parameters too. **B**: only a *read* is a use — the initialiser is not a write, a field/element write writes the root (closing the hole Go left open at golang/go#20802, free here because §4.10 has no aliases), and a write through an `@` parameter IS a use (§4.8's copy-out is the whole observable effect). **C**: the two-tier built-in namespace is WITHDRAWN — one tier, every name in §4.20's inventory reserved everywhere including `extern`; the tier field survives as documentation of where the implementation comes from, rebuilt from §1.11/§4.20 (`range` is Tier 2, `.str()` and `sort` were missing, `panic`/`Builder` deliberately excluded). **D**: resolve UFCS names at M3a but report an unknown one only when no record or variant in the file declares a field of that name. **E**: sibling scopes may reuse a name, and the enclosing-shadow check is SEQUENTIAL — a deliberate departure from Java JLS §6.4 and C# CS0136, both order-insensitive, on §1.3 (a line below must not decide whether the line above compiles). Net spec cost 0 | panel 015: C died from two directions — the ergonomist vetoed the silent override on locality (`xs.map(f)`'s meaning would depend on a distant line) and the warden on Principle 0 (an exception to a rule the spec already states, +23/+48 measured); D's rejection half was VETOED by the engineer with a compiled counterexample (`h.cb(n)` on a function-valued field, legal under §4.13, rejected twice by its prototype) and survives only narrowed; A's escape valve was missing and three judges converged on it (Zig is the only shipped precedent and all six enforcing languages ship `_`; the `sqlite3_exec` trampoline needs ≥2 unread parameters). Measured: 4215 C headers scanned, zero collisions with the reserved list; the witness has 53 UFCS calls, not 23 | §4.4, §4.16, §4.11, §4.20, §1.11, §1.3 | 015

2026-08-04 | `heroes check` is the frontend command from M3a on, and golden `check/` runs through it instead of `parse`; the resolver runs only when lexing and parsing produced nothing, so a broken file reports exactly what it reported before | the stage the goldens run through moves with the deepest frontend stage (DESIGN-LOG 2026-08-04); the name now stops moving — M3b–M3d deepen `check` without renaming it | CLAUDE.md §9 | — |

2026-08-04 | `heroes fmt` gains two policies, both of them "the layout the author chose is content": a run of consecutive single-line `match` arms **aligns its `=>`** (the run stops at a block-bodied arm and at a blank line), and a **list literal written across lines stays across lines** even when it would fit on one (§4.9 gives the two forms different separators, so joining it deletes a grouping rather than rewrapping it). A blank line between arms now survives, like a blank line between statements. design.md's appendix is reformatted to canonical form and a test now asserts it byte for byte — the reference aesthetic and the tool's output are the same artifact | author instruction (`/goal`, "comincia a pensare all'ergonomia"), applied to the formatter first because the appendix had been hand-aligning its arms since before any compiler existed. Two defects found by the new tests, both mine, both in the blank-line bookkeeping: a spurious blank line after every multi-line list, and — on the first attempt at the fix — a `last_line` reaching past a `Dedent` and stealing the next declaration's doc comment, which is the bug panel 014 had already fixed once and whose comment warned about exactly this | §4.15, §4.9, §4.1 | — |
2026-08-04 | `examples/gallery/` born: nine programs written to be read (records · variant+match · `T?` · loops · `@` copy-out · generics · strings · FFI · holes), each held to four properties by the suite — parses clean, resolves clean, canonical byte for byte, tree preserved by formatting | author instruction (`/goal`): "così comincio a dare un'occhiata estetica". All nine resolved on the first attempt, which is the datum: the resolver's rules do not fight code written without the resolver in mind. 2026-08-04 | Type-system edges settled (panel 017, RATIFIED same day by author instruction). **A**: a jump (`break`/`continue`/`return`) has **no type** — the engineer built all three options and measured that typing one at all rejects every function ending in `return` (RFC 1216's wrong turn, reproduced); adopted with the historian's withdrawal condition met, a **single shared join routine** (`types/join.rs`) rather than a rule per construct, and **no bottom type** in the table so M5c's descriptor pass never meets an uninhabited type. design.md §4.7's two bullets contradicted each other on `tag = match t` with a `break` arm and are amended: "every arm must produce a value **or jump**", with the value rule's payoff re-attributed to a `()`-typed arm body, where the silent program actually was. **B**: `+` joins two `str` (concatenation, no implicit conversion), and the int-to-string conversion enters the inventory as a DEFECT FIX named **`to_str`** — §4.20 had it as `.str()`, the spec omitted it entirely, and without it the language cannot render a number into a message (the ergonomist produced the string from spec v0 in 28 lines with two silent failure modes). **C**: a `()` value cannot be bound — Nim's rule verbatim, in the language CLAUDE.md §6 copies the surface of; the fix is `Guess`, not `Certain`, because a `Fix` carries one span and deleting the binding would orphan the read. **D**: a declaration as an inline arm body is rejected **in the parser**, where Rust puts it, because in the checker the resolver speaks first and the user gets two diagnostics for one mistake | panel 017 | §4.7, §4.14, §4.20, §1.11, §4.3 | 017
2026-08-04 | `heroes check` runs three stages in order — lex+parse, resolve, check — each only if the one before it said nothing; and the acceptance program now **type-checks clean**, which found a twelfth `T`-where-`T?` site panels 002/006 had missed (`tokenize`'s `return out`). Two soundness holes found by the checker's own tests: a value block ending on a *statement* was treated as if it diverged (so `x = if c` with a loop in the branch bound `x` to nothing), and binding a `()` reported twice | M3b/M3c implementation | §4.5, §4.7 | 017
2026-08-04 | `heroes` command surface fixed (panel 016, RATIFIED same day by author instruction): design.md §3.5's spellings implemented verbatim (`lex --dump-tokens`, `parse --dump-ast`, `check --dump-scopes`, `--dump-ir` reserved for M4) and every stage now prints **only when asked**; `--json` says *how*, never *what*; the mutating flag is `--in-place` (`--write`/`-w` retired, each naming its replacement, as the language does for `fn`); a three-value exit contract **0 clean / 1 the input has diagnostics / 2 the tool could not run** printed in `--help` and asserted by the golden harness; **one strict table-driven argv parser** with an `enum Tag` (a table entry with no dispatch arm is a compile error) and an unknown-flag error that *enumerates* what is accepted; `--emit-c` stays an output, outside the `--dump-*` family; `measure` states its default. 2026-08-04 | **ASan cannot see leaks on this platform** — `detect_leaks is not supported on this platform`, exit 134, measured by two judges independently, then demonstrated: 999 leaked blocks, ASan build exit 0 in silence. So the sanitiser configuration stays for use-after-free and double-free (it caught two real bugs in the panel's own C on their first run) and **stops being described as a leak detector**; the leak gate is `hero_runtime_live()`/`hero_runtime_check_leaks()`, a live-block counter asserted at exit — 12 lines, portable, deterministic, naming a count instead of a stack. Shape: a **flag**, `--sanitize`, on `build` and `run` (same question, same input, different codegen) | the milestone that introduces reference counting would otherwise have shipped with no leak detector at all, behind a configuration that looked like one | §3.1, CLAUDE.md §9, §10 | 021 |
2026-08-05 | **The ownership pass moves, it does not release.** An owning temporary is *moved* into a synthetic slot in the block that defines it, so nothing is owned by a temporary past its own instruction, nothing owned crosses a block edge, and every release is a slot's — at a point already known. Panel 021's costing (release at the end of the defining block, made safe by a checked invariant) was refuted by that very invariant on two real programs within the hour: `.must()`, `?`, `&&` and `if`-as-an-expression all open a block in the *middle* of an expression, so `name + " scored " + got.must().to_str()` builds a concatenation in one block and consumes it in another. The consequence is a uniform reading — **every value that reaches a store is borrowed** — and the cost is over-retention, not a leak, because a loop's synthetic slot is overwritten each iteration | the alternative was liveness, which is the analysis that choosing slots over phi nodes (panel 019) existed to avoid; this cashes that decision properly | Part 5, §4.10 | 019, 021 |
2026-08-05 | `ir/asserts.rs` routes a **counted** assert operand through a synthetic slot. The `assert` lowering computes both comparison operands in the test block and reads them in the abort block — which is what lets the message quote values — and for a `str` that is a block-crossing owned reference. Panel 021 predicted the collision and scheduled the repair for M6 with `Abort::Assert`; the verifier's `Owned` invariant fired on design.md's own gallery first | two instructions on a path that is about to abort the program, against a use-after-free in the one construct whose whole job is to report accurately | §4.18 | 021 |
2026-08-05 | The check that a returning block releases every slot it owns is keyed on **position**: the sweep's load *follows* the last store to that slot, where a store's old-value load necessarily *precedes* it. Two earlier forms failed — counting `Decref`s was a proxy that stopped meaning anything when the pass changed shape, and pairing a `Decref` with a `Load` of the same slot could not tell a sweep from a store because the two pairs are byte-identical. Found by deleting a sweep decref by hand and watching the check stay silent | an invariant that cannot fail on a hand-made violation is not an invariant | Part 10 step 6, CLAUDE.md §9 | 021 |

2026-08-05 | **Panel 022 — COW is per-step, not per-primitive.** One unshare for every array or map step of a mutated place, each writing back at its level, and the mutation primitives take `HeroArrayHeader **` rather than returning the new pointer. Measured: with a single unshare at the primitive, `h = g` then `g.rows[0].cells[0] @ 7` changes `h` too — ASan clean, leak counter zero, exit 0, a green harness on a program that violates spec line 58 ("No aliasing exists anywhere"). It is *necessarily* wrong, not accidentally: unsharing level 1 copies its elements, whose `copy` increfs level 2, so level 2 is shared exactly when level 1 was copied. The order is part of the rule — the stored value is increfed **before** the outermost unshare | nothing in this project could have caught it: the leak counter reads 0, the sanitisers are silent, and no golden read a second name after a nested mutation | §4.10, Part 5 | 022 |

Kept inside the runtime primitive, C's own rule that all arguments exist before the callee's first statement makes the bad order **inexpressible** — which is better than a check, because a check can be forgotten | the same instruction was struck at M5b for having no call sites; it now has one and still does not belong in the IR | Part 5, §4.10 | 021, 022 |

2026-08-05 | The descriptor pass may **never** use `memcmp` or hash bytes: `record Flag { n: int, on: bool }` carries 7 padding bytes, so two `==`-equal records hash differently with no compile error and no sanitiser report. And `hash` is generated for **every** type, never null — a call through a null `hash` is `SEGV on unknown address 0x0, pc 0x0`, with no type name and no source line. Go's cheaper rule (emit `hash` only for map-key types) reintroduces the null and is rejected on that ground. A payload-free variant case is omitted from the union, because C11 has no empty struct. 2026-08-05 | **Cycles are impossible, and the published reason is not the one the proposal gave.** Racordon, Shabalin, Zheng, **Abrahams** and Saeta, "Native Implementation of Mutable Value Semantics" (ICOOOLPS '21, arXiv 2106.12678), contains four of this proposal's six points in four pages — synthesized per-type copy/destroy, refcounted array blocks, a mutation-time counter check, and no cycle collector — and its guarantee is *"references cannot be stored in variables or object fields, hence all values form disjoint topological trees"*, a statement about what a field may hold rather than about copying terminating. Two exceptions named: §4.19's `ptr`/`cstr`, and a stored closure, which Heroes forbids. The paper is **stricter** than Heroes (it forbids recursive types outright, where Heroes carries finiteness through `[T]`) and the departure is recorded. Swift has shipped twelve years the same way; Nim's ORC is gated on `canFormAcycle` and motivated by closures. The queued cycle question closes by citation, and `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s "reference counting leaks cycles" is corrected | a right answer with a wrong reason is one refactor away from being a wrong answer | §4.10 gift 2 | 021, 022 |

2026-08-10 | **The value-semantics choice is recorded with its lineage and its rejected alternatives** (§4.10), because the failure mode is not disagreement but *repair*: an implementer who reads no references, no graphs, copies everywhere as a beginner's mistake will keep trying to fix it. Six alternatives priced in a table (shared-reference RC, ARC/ORC, tracing GC, borrow checker, manual allocators, C++ RAII); five appendix entries promoted from names to arguments (APL/J/K/BQN split in **opposite directions** — against on notation, in favour on the value model, with kdb+'s 10-of-17 STAC-M3 Antuco records as the evidence that the model is not what makes a language slow; R/MATLAB as precedent *and* warning, their slowness being Heroes' own O(n)-per-mutation bill; Erlang with its motive inverted; Swift as the half taken and the half declined; Hylo as the completeness argument). Three claims in the author's draft were **not** adopted: `null`'s absence is §4.6's, not value semantics'; "no data races by construction" holds only once Part 7.13's heaps exist, so it is stated as *inexpressible after*, not *bought now*; and "nothing is ever genuinely shared" contradicts COW, whose whole premise is physical sharing — the true statement is *across threads*. Honest summary adopted verbatim: **strong at transforming data, awkward at interlinking data** | zero spec tokens and zero code: this is the section most likely to be "improved" by someone who has not read the trade | §4.10, appendix | — |
2026-08-10 | **Part 7.13 gets its shape** — isolated per-thread heaps, a mailbox per thread, and **any Heroes value as a message** with no envelope and no serialisation format, which falls straight out of §4.10. Two copy regimes that are not a contradiction (COW within a thread, unconditional at the boundary) and are not to be unified. Green threads stay in Part 7 rather than joining coroutines in Part 6, because the vertex that says no is **simplicity** — a scheduler — and this Part's own preamble makes that a deferral; the operative rule is the same either way: **do not build a scheduler**. The Erlang precedent is recorded with its correction: refc binaries over 64 bytes and literals are **not** copied between processes but shared under a reference count, so the escape hatch for large payloads is a cross-heap refcount, i.e. an atomic counter — the one cost this design avoids, to be traded consciously and not rediscovered | design intent written down so the implementation is mechanical rather than a redesign, and because two consequences bind today's code | Part 7.13, §4.20 | — |

2026-08-10 | **Panel 023 — a type with no size.** New diagnostic class `no_size` in the **checker** (a diagnostic `heroes check` cannot see is in the wrong pass — `missing_return`'s own precedent), and the rule is a **property, not a list**: *an edge is a field C lays out by value*. Stated as "a cycle not through `[T]` or `{K: V}`" the proposal was wrong in both directions and both were compiled, not argued: `record Scope { vars: {str: Scope} }` is legal at 8 bytes, and `record R { next: R? }` is `field has incomplete type` at exit 1, because a `T?` is by value and therefore **transparent** — it propagates the edge into `T`. Two judges found the `T?` row independently from opposite ends, one reading only the spec (`next: Node?` is "the most attractive wrong answer" for a list) and one reading only the tree. The property also forced itself: the check and the emitter's `typedef` order are one relation (§3.1:461, ordered since before the backend existed), and two edge sets cannot be one walk — a map imposes **no** ordering constraint on C, measured, three records mutually recursive through arrays compiling in the worst order with zero forward declarations. So `types/sized.rs` publishes the order in `Checked` and `emit/` contains no graph code. The walk is **iterative**: rustc #84611 is a stack overflow inside this very check, Nim #13715 a SIGSEGV, Zig #21436 an undetected cycle plus a crash — the check that prevents a hang is the one that historically hangs | today all four illegal shapes exit **0**, and the day M5c deletes the gate row they become exit 2 with a path to generated C: this compiler telling the author the compiler is broken about the author's mistake | §3.1, §4.17, §4.20 | 023 |

Swift is the only precedent in Heroes' position — a value type with no escape hatch — and its own Sema tests put the error on the **field**, not the type header, with the cycle as a named path in a note. Heroes takes one diagnostic per cycle at the closing field, one note per edge naming `Type.field` and its line, because in `A → B → A` both edges are legal repair sites and naming one silently is arbitrary advice dressed as help; Go's old `cmd/compile` printed only `invalid recursive type T2` for a mutual pair and that was **filed as a bug** (#41575). The +40 bullet was rejected twice over — it was **false** about `{K: V}`, and its "holding itself *directly*" re-licensed the mutual recursion spec line 38 explicitly invites; the corrected form measured +49 for the same falsehood. What the +6 buys is not the case the proposal was about: reading only the spec, the ergonomist put **~75% confidence on `children: [Node]` being legal at all**, because line 60's "No aliasing exists anywhere" reads as *no indirection anywhere* and the only in-spec support is line 97's `s.children`, an example whose types are never written down — predicting that 10–20% of samples hand-roll an index arena to dodge a restriction that does not exist. 2026-08-10 | **A record is a C struct by value and a variant a tagged union by value**, and the per-type functions C cannot write are generated: `retain`/`release` (typed, so every call the emitter writes is checked) plus `eq` and `hash` walking **fields, never bytes**. A payload struct gets a real name because `Ty::Case` is a real type in the IR — `$t5: Token.num = payload $t4 .num` puts one in a temporary and a temporary needs a declaration. A payload-free case leaves the union, and when *every* case is payload-free the union goes away entirely: `union { }` compiles at `-Wall` and is `error: empty union is a GNU extension` under `-pedantic-errors`, which §4.19 schedules. The compound literal is **designated**, redundantly, because the IR already holds the arguments in order — which is exactly why: a field inserted mid-record becomes a clang error at every construction site instead of shifting every value one field along | verified by compiling the generated C with `-pedantic-errors`, so panel 023's prediction 9 is pre-empted rather than left to fire | §4.2, §4.10, §4.20 | 022, 023 |
2026-08-10 | **Ownership rule 6: an aggregate constructor retains every counted field it captures.** A record is by value, so `Pair(one: p, two: q)` copies the `str` handle inside `p` and the new value is a second owner of one block; rule 5 moves the *result* into a slot, which is about the aggregate. `ok(x)` is deliberately excluded — wrapping consumes its argument rather than sharing it — so the shapes are listed one at a time. **And rule 3 reads the stored value's type, not the root slot's**: for a whole-slot store the two questions coincide, which is why it was right for two milestones, and `p.cells @ [7, 8]` is where they part — the slot is a `Row`, counted because it *contains* an array, while the place written is the `[int]`. Both defects printed every correct answer and were wrong once, at the sweep | found by the live-block counter, twice; neither is visible in any output at any input | §4.10, Part 5 | 021, 022 |
2026-08-10 | **`push` copies, always**, and it is a correctness decision wearing a performance decision's clothes. `xs = [1, 2, 3]` leaves `xs` observable and its slot holds one reference, so a refcount of 1 means "only the slot has it" and appending in place would change what the slot sees — value semantics has no reading in which the argument is consumed. Building an array by successive push is therefore O(n²), the same bill §4.10 already declared for `s + t`. Recorded because the cheap in-place path *looks* available whenever the count is 1, and that is precisely when it is not | the IR cannot know that the result replaces the argument, and a rule that depends on knowing it is a rule that fails silently | §4.10, §4.20 | 022 |
2026-08-10 | **One `HeroDesc` serves every `[T]`**, whatever `T` is: `copy`, `drop`, `eq` and `hash` on an array value all reach the element type through the header's own `elem`, so `[[int]]` and `[[str]]` share one row. The generated set is a **reachability worklist** over the program's arrays, not a walk over declarations, because a `static const` descriptor nothing points at is `-Wunused-const-variable` — and it is emitted **before** the function definitions, since an array literal names its element's descriptor. A nested aggregate's `hash` calls its own function rather than going through a descriptor: `record Rect { corner: Point }` hashes a `Point` no array holds | three of the milestone's six defects were this class — an ordering or a spelling that clang caught, against three that only an instrument would have | §4.20 | 022 |
2026-08-10 | **Copy-on-write is one unshare per array step of the place, with write-back, and the primitives take `HeroArrayHeader **`** — the double pointer is the design, because a caller cannot forget to store a result that does not exist. `hero_array_set` unshares, releases the element that was there, and **moves** the value in, so the ownership pass increfs before the store and does not decref after. That order is the rule: the incref precedes the outermost unshare because the value may live inside the container being copied, which is what `n.children[0] @ n` builds — and what comes out is a tree, because the captured value refers to the old block and the unshare made a new one. `Op::CowCheck` stays out of the IR: inside the primitive, C's own rule that every argument is evaluated before the callee's first statement makes the hoisted order **inexpressible**, which beats a check because a check can be forgotten | panel 022's veto now runs: `h = g` then `g.rows[0].cells[0] @ 7` prints 7 and 0, where the single-unshare version printed 7 and 7 with every instrument in this project reporting success | §4.10, §4.20 | 021, 022 |
2026-08-10 | The **read-only map and `T?`'s representation are deferred out of M5c**, and the reason is Principle 0 rather than time. Panel 022 already struck the map's mutation half for having zero reachable call sites, and its R4 leaves the larger question open: `{K: V}` may be **deleted from the language** at M6's closure audit, measured at −57 spec tokens against +29 to fund `set` plus `for k in m`. Building the container's ~180 lines of C, plus a generated option struct per `T?`, before that answer is exactly what `cow_check` was struck for at M5b — with more force, because here the whole type may go | a milestone that ships a form the audit may delete has spent its budget on a decision it did not make | §1.0, §4.9, §4.20 | 022 |
2026-08-10 | **`T?` is a tagged union by value, and every one of them is reference-counted whatever `T` is** — `int?` included, because its error side is `HeroFailure`, two `str`s. `HeroFailure` ships in the runtime rather than being generated: it is the one record every `T?` in every program contains and §4.6 fixes its shape, so there is no declaration to generate it from. One `typedef` per distinct `T?`, named by **index** rather than by spelling, because `int?` and `[int]?` sanitise to the same identifier and a collision there is two types sharing one C name; the tag is a plain `int64_t`, since `Op::Tag`'s result is an `int` and lowering already compares it against `const 0`. Correction to an older note, chronological rather than a change of mind: `retains_fields` had `ok(x)` as a *wrap* that consumes its argument, on panel 021's wording — rule 5 came later and made every value reaching a constructor borrowed, so consuming one is a release the program never made | the note was written before the rule that invalidates it, which is the failure mode of a comment that records a decision instead of an invariant | §4.6, §4.20 | 021, 022, 023 |
2026-08-10 | **The map is open addressing over three parallel regions with a FIXED SEED, and `==` ignores order by construction.** Parallel regions — a state byte per bucket, then keys, then values — rather than a struct per bucket, because element sizes come from descriptors at runtime and a per-bucket layout would need padding the runtime computes. The seed is fixed because iteration order must be a function of the contents: the self-hosting fixpoint compares generated C byte for byte (panel 006), and a per-run seed makes the compiler emit two different correct outputs so the fixpoint never closes. Equality is "same length, every entry found in the other" rather than a pairwise walk of two entry arrays, which would make `{"a":1,"b":2}` and `{"b":2,"a":1}` unequal — spec line 58, and the row panel 022 ranked first among what a cheap map gets silently wrong. `m[k]` yields a `V?` and the runtime hands back an address or NULL, because it cannot build an option struct that is generated per payload type | `hash` finally has a caller, two milestones after panel 022 required it non-null | §4.9, §4.20 | 006, 022 |

Found by the leak counter on `{1: "one" + "!"}` followed by `.default(…)` — exactly one block, and **only with a computed value**, because a `str` literal has a negative refcount and the same missing decref is invisible. The consequence for every test in this project that touches counting: allocate on purpose, `"one" + "!"` rather than `"one"` | the arm's own comment had said "listed rather than defaulted so that switching one on is a decision here", and this is that decision | Part 5, §4.20 | 021, 022 |

2026-08-10 | **Panel 006's insertion-order guarantee is withdrawn as FALSE, not weakened.** `HeroMapHeader` has no order field, so arrival is **not stored** and no iteration strategy can recover it; and `cap` derives from a literal's **duplicate-inclusive** pair count, so two `==`-equal maps written differently iterate differently — measured: `{"a":1,"b":2,"c":3}` iterates `b a c` at cap 8, the same map with a duplicated first key iterates `b c a` at cap 16. The fixed seed stays: determinism across runs is still a fixpoint requirement. Nothing pins that number today | a budget whose veto is out of reach is decoration; the cheapest instrument that makes it fire is not always a capability | §1.6, CLAUDE.md §9, §10 | 031, 024 |

2026-08-12 | **Subdirectories: refused for now, and the reason is a count rather than a preference.** Author instruction pulled the decision forward from M10 ("se no il self host parte molto disordinato"); the panel answers the *reason* instead of the request. **A path buys zero `use` lines** — 452 flat, 452 nested, because a path changes how a target is spelled and never whether a `use` is needed — and it forces the **same renames**: 40 of the port's 119 files sit in 15 last-part collision groups (`decls` ×5, `types` ×5, `exprs` ×4), every one refused by the proposal's own uniqueness clause, and repaired by `types/typedecls.hero` qualified `typedecls.foo()`, which is flat naming with directories painted on. **Nim's compiler has run 171 files in one directory for years** under the same last-component rule, and Go's compiler was 60,000 lines before it was split into packages, *after* self-hosting. The path form stays purely additive — `module_path_has_no_parts` refuses every `/` today — so waiting is free and acting pre-empts M10, where packages produce the case that decides the spelling. **If the author overrules, the form is fixed at +26** (`- A `use` may be a path: `use syntax/decl` binds `decl`. Last parts are unique.`), never the +38 tabled | Principle 0's burden is not met — subdirectories are not on the closure list, no Part 11 effect exists, and "the port would be tidier" is the shape measurement 003 rider 3 refused when this file's own ROADMAP scheduled `outline` and `explain` | §1.0, §1.3, §1.6 | 032 |
2026-08-12 | **"A directory is a module" is struck, by three independent vetoes and one opposition.** It is the only candidate in the panel's record that produces a **silent wrong program**: its wording makes `use syntax` mean one thing while `syntax.hero` is absent and another once it exists, so adding a file — a facade, a generated output, an unfinished rename — silently rebinds every call in every importer, and both versions type-check. Compiled, it also chooses a translation unit's **headers by directory membership**: an ncurses binding and a raylib binding in one `ui/` directory give 8 errors inside `raylib.h` from a library the program never calls, where the per-file form is exit 0. It buys 165 fewer `use` lines and buys them by deleting "always qualified" (panel 030 R4, panel 031) and adding a directory-enumeration primitive the closure list does not license. TypeScript ran the experiment: its namespaces are this model, its team spent 2019–2023 removing them, and the migration published a 46% smaller package and 10–25% faster builds | the conditional clause is panel 023's falsified form verbatim, and this project's own log calls a rule smuggled into a subordinate clause the highest-risk text in the document | §1.3, §4.19 | 032 |
2026-08-12 | **The C symbol component is the module's whole path, sanitised and concatenated — never its last part.** Measured over the port's 169 real paths: last-part components collide **22-way on `mod`** alone, whole-path concatenation collides **zero** times. This holds whether or not paths ever enter the language, and it is the shape `module_names_collide` must compare | the two namespaces are not the same namespace: what the author types before the dot and what reaches the linker have different collision sets, and one check was written over the wrong one | §3.1, CLAUDE.md §7 | 032 |
2026-08-12 | **`use` is scoped to the program, and the spec has to say so before there is a unit above the file.** OCaml's flat module namespace is survivable inside one program and unfixable across libraries, which is why Dune's wrapped libraries exist and why the fix had to come from the build system rather than the language. Heroes has no bundle yet; when M10 adds packages, a uniqueness rule that inherited a global scope by silence is the one that cannot be re-scoped | a rule whose scope was never stated acquires the widest one by default, and that is the scope nobody chose | §1.0, Part 7 item 4 | 032 |

2026-08-12 | **Private record fields and private variant cases are struck permanently, and move onto Part 6.** Fields were already refused by name at design.md:1142 inside §4.9's prose; the refusal now sits on the list that means *never*. Three vetoes each, from three inputs: a private field is an **opaque type wearing a field annotation's name** (`check_named_fields` demands every field, so the type becomes unconstructible from outside, and `types/access.rs:42,66` would print the hidden field's name in the message refusing it); the precedented form hides the *type* and never the field (Haskell 2010 §5.2's abstract datatypes, Ada 83's private part with no methods for twelve years, Zig issue 9909 closed not planned); and — compiled — **privacy stops at the ABI**: a §4.19-style shim read the "private" field at offset 16 of 24 bytes, wrote it, and built the "private" case, with `nm -gU` showing that case's `eq`/`hash` as global `T` symbols. For cases, Rust ran the experiment: `priv` variants shipped, were **deleted** by RFC 0026 (2014) as rarely used, returned per-*type* as `#[non_exhaustive]` in 1.40.0 (Dec 2019), and Rust's own documentation records the price — *"exhaustiveness checking cannot be used"* — with the repair lint unstable since 2021 | Heroes cannot pay that price at all: `_` is forbidden on variants, so a hidden case makes **whether a line compiles depend on a declaration the reader is forbidden to read**, and `types/patterns.rs:1-15` says that coupling is the entire reason the ban exists | §4.7, §4.9, §4.20, Part 6 | 033 |
2026-08-12 | **Declaration visibility is neither adopted nor refused: it becomes a Part 7 item whose decision instrument is M8p, and its landing form is fixed at +18, not the tabled +30.** Cost was never the objection — ~80 non-test lines, **zero** in `ir/`, `own.rs`, `emit/` and the mangler, because one whole-program translation unit gives visibility no linkage consequence; +30 is 1.8% of headroom. The burden is. It is not on §1.0's closure list, no Part 11 effect exists, and **the §1-derived argument offered was refuted by a grep**: Heroes compiles one whole program, so a declaration nobody calls is already computable — `unused_use` (`resolve/top.rs:119`) proves that shape at zero tokens, and privacy only makes the check module-local, which starts mattering at M9. The priced text is also not the justifying text: the bare licence measures **+18**, licence plus the dead-code rule **+27**, and the tabled +30 buys a prohibition clause that R1 puts on Part 6 for free. No shipped language makes an unused top-level declaration a hard error — rustc's `dead_code` is defined on visibility but warn-by-default, Go's spec stops at the function body, OCaml's warning 32 is off, Nim's is a hint whose error form crashed the compiler until 1.6.14 — and every one shipped an escape valve | ROADMAP:602's rule applies literally, *a form whose workaround compiles is a Part 7 deferral by default*, and the workaround compiles: `examples/calculator/` is four modules with zero visibility. Deciding now would also convert panel 031's registered ≥+25 prediction into one this panel fulfilled by voting for it — the warden's finding, and the reason the direction (256 `private` against 355 `export` over the port's own tree) is left to the count M8p produces | §1.0, §1.7, Part 7 | 033, 031 |
2026-08-12 | **M9's extern row gets its own rule, and it is not a visibility rule: an `extern` is never callable across a module boundary.** Panel 030 R2 row 1 offered a disjunct — the header travels into every calling TU, *or* externs are module-private and the type checker refuses the qualified call — and this was the only place the record needed visibility at all. Compiled, **neither tier delivers it**: opt-in leaves public externs as the default, and default-private keeps `export`, which is the common case for a binding module. Three shapes built against real headers: header in both TUs is exit 1 in both; header only in the declaring TU is exit 1 there and **exit 0 in the caller with the wrong signature** (`rc=0 db=open`); extern module-private with a Heroes wrapper has nothing to get wrong. `private` also cannot *mean* C `static` — `static declaration of 'sqrt' follows non-static declaration` against the real header — and the fallback of dropping the `#include` links to the real global symbol and runs | the rule is smaller than any visibility tier, spends **zero** tokens, and satisfies row 1 whether or not visibility ever ships; it belongs to M9's acceptance rows rather than to this panel, which was convened about the language | §4.19, Part 10 | 033, 030 |
2026-08-12 | **The Rust shape is already in the language, and the record now says so; what Rust has and Heroes does not is the *typed* error, which stays a Part 8 wart rather than becoming a Part 7 item.** `T?` ≈ `Result<T,E>`, `expr?` ≈ `?`, `.must()` ≈ `.unwrap()`, `.default(v)` ≈ `.unwrap_or(v)`, `match .ok/.err`, exceptions refused permanently. Typed errors need three things this language has decided against — **generics on types** (design.md:1425 is a positive rule, not an omission), traits for any conversion (Part 7 item 8), and a runtime tax — and Part 7's preamble admits only items that *"lose only on the simplicity vertex"*, so filing them there would turn a wart into a promise. Measured against real `sqlite3.h`: a typed error carrying what a C library actually reports (`sqlite3_errmsg`, copied through `hero_str_from_bytes`) is **32 bytes and refcounted**, and `ptr?SqlErr` is **40** — identical to today; the 16-byte win needs a payload-free error, which cannot carry a message. Compiled, `?` across two bindings stops being a struct copy (`error: assigning to 'h_AppErr' from incompatible type 'h_SqlErr'`) and needs one hand-written converter per pair, and `m[k]` has no error type to be | Swift shipped typed throws (SE-0413, accepted 2023-12-20, Swift 6.0) a decade after 1.0, **with** full generics, and its own proposal says *"Resist the temptation to use typed throws"*; Rust's landing place is a derive macro over an enum, which needs generics on types, traits and macros — three refusals | §4.6, §4.12, Part 8 wart 5 | 034 |
2026-08-12 | **The stringly-typed error code is a measured defect class, not acknowledged debt — and the cure is a `constant`, which the language has had since M2.** `heroes mutate`'s new `typo-code` operator reads **25 mutants, 0 killed, in both arms**, the only zero in an eleven-row table. The published magnitude: Eghbali & Pradel, ASE '20, 204 real string bugs — **42% are incorrect string literals**, a widely used analyser finds **1 of 204**, and **11%** ever surface as an error message. The proposed cure, a diagnostic refusing a comparison against a code no `fail` constructs, is **not adopted**: it is root-dependent (the same file errors rooted at `lex` and is clean rooted at `main` — compiled), it rejects the correct program `e.code == "missing_key"` written straight from spec line 133, `fail(prefix() + "key", msg)` compiles today so its escape hatch is a silent program-wide off-switch, and it cannot fire in the self-hosted compiler at all, whose codes are a record field threaded through `@diags`. Adopted instead, at zero spec tokens and zero compiler lines: **codes as `constant`s**, compared as `store.ERR_UNKNOWN_ITEM`, where a typo is `error[unknown_name]` today, locally, with the owning module named | the sting is the finding: the convention **silently defeats the diagnostic**, because there is no literal at the comparison left to check — *the rule is strongest where the code is worst and silent where it is best*. And no shipped compiler makes an *inferred* closed-world string domain a hard error: TypeScript's domain is declared, Rust's `unexpected_cfgs` is a warning because `build.rs` can widen it, Dialyzer lives outside the compiler | §4.6, §1.3, Part 11 | 034, 031 |
2026-08-12 | **A variant case payload was never released when no `match` in the same compilation bound it — the same module leaked rooted at itself and was clean rooted at its caller.** `emit/perfn.rs::owns_case` asked the type interner, and `Ty::Case(decl, case)` is interned when a `match` arm **binds** the payload, not when the case is built. `examples/calculator/lex.hero` constructs `.name(s: …)` and never destructures it, so it emitted `void h_lex_Token_release(…) { switch (v->tag) { default: break; } }` — an empty switch — while the identical declaration compiled beside `parse.hero` emitted the walking release. The retain was emitted either way. Fix: ask the **fields**, which do not move between compilations, where the interner does | 90 `run/` cases missed it because the shape needs a payload owning a *computed* `str` (a literal is static and cannot leak) **and** no `match` binding it in the same compilation — every existing case that builds a `str`-carrying variant also destructures one. No golden's emitted C changed when it was fixed, which is the same finding said twice | §4.10, §4.20, CLAUDE.md §7 | 034 |
2026-08-12 | **Eighteen panel sessions ratified in one blanket author instruction**, from 013 (the function type's marker, open since M2) through 034, plus the Part 6/7 split for green threads. Each panel file gains a `## Ratification` section that says what the instruction was — *"ratifica anche tutto quello che c'è da ratificare"*, given after a session summary rather than a clause-by-clause review — and states the limit: it settles that the provisional resolution **is** the decision and that work no longer runs on it as a default; it does not settle anything a panel keyed to a measurement that has not been taken (metric 2 above all), because a blanket yes cannot make a number arrive | the alternative was worse in both directions: eighteen sessions whose resolutions have been load-bearing since the day they landed, still labelled `provisional — author ratification pending`, is a record that understates what the compiler actually assumes — and a ratification recorded as if it were eighteen individual reviews would overstate it. Writing down which one it was costs three sentences and keeps CLAUDE.md §12's rule intact where it is easiest to break: in the project's own account of itself | Part 0, CLAUDE.md §4 | 013, 019–034 |
2026-08-12 | **Nine emitter defects, one shape, and the shape is D3's: the emitter kept asking what the program *mentioned* where the answer had to come from what a declaration *is*.** Every one refused or miscompiled an ordinary program, and every one was found by compiling rather than reading. `equality_body` had **no row** for `Ty::Map`, `Ty::Fallible` or `Ty::Func` while `hash_body` covered all three, so a record holding a map hashed fine and **aborted on `==`** — a map keyed by one inserts and then dies on lookup, which is CLAUDE.md §7's "eq and hash agree" broken in the loudest direction. `descriptors::generated()` answered "which types did the program mention inside a container" while its three callers ask "which types does the function I am about to write name": `function grab() -> P?` was `use of undeclared identifier 'h_M_P_desc'` at exit 2, and adding `[P]` *anywhere in the program* fixed it. Declared aggregates were emitted wholesale before the typedefs the emitter invents, and **each kind can contain the other** — `record Box { v: int? }` needs the option first, `function grab() -> P?` needs the record first, both ordinary. `builtins::reachable` matched `Op::Call` and never `Op::FuncRef`, so a library function passed as a **value** was referenced and never defined. The emitter's own `opt<N>`/`fn<N>` names were unreserved, so `record opt0` gave `redefinition`. And a `()` field reached clang as `void f_u;`, because the gate walks the IR and a declaration's field list is in none of it | **no golden moved and no expectation changed** — the corpus was not compiling any of these shapes, which is the same finding as D3's ninety `run/` cases and the reason each fix ships with a case named after it. The typedef repair is one containment order over both kinds, seeded with the two old orders so a program without cross-kind containment emits byte-identically; the name repair uses a **leading digit**, which `h_<component>_<name>` can carry and a Heroes identifier cannot, closing panel 031 R10's residual by construction instead of by a check | §4.2, §4.6, §4.13, §4.20, CLAUDE.md §7, §10 | 031, 034 |
2026-08-12 | **A narrowing asks the value, never the world** (author instruction, after sweep 001). Two of the sweep's twenty defects were protected by a comment that *argued correctly for a world that had changed*: `spans_lines` was narrow on purpose, with the reason written out — a reason about source **a person writes**, which stopped being true the day the 88-column rule made the formatter write source too; and `case_fields_are_counted` returned `false` under a valid argument from a premise that had died. Both had been read by reviewers who agreed with them, which is the whole finding: **a convention about comment style cannot catch this, because the comment is not wrong.** What can catch it is the shape of the question. Both repairs turned out to be the same one — ask the value's own extent, ask the declaration's own fields, instead of asking what kind of thing people write or what the program happened to mention. A fact about the value in hand cannot expire | the rule is now CLAUDE.md §11, with two obligations where a premise is unavoidable: state it as a falsifiable claim rather than a justification, and give it a **test that fires when it dies**, whose failure message names its dependants. `a_declared_type_cannot_contain_a_type_parameter` is the first — §4.12's "generics on functions only" is silently load-bearing in `emit/ctype.rs::mentions_generic`, `ir/mono.rs::contains` and `types/table.rs::poisoned`, all three of which walk `Array\|Fallible\|Map\|Func` and answer `false` for a `Ty::Named`. Nothing connected the three to the rule, and the rule itself is enforced **by accident**: a type parameter is unresolvable in a field because the parameter list lives on the function, not because anyone wrote "a declaration may not be generic". Part 8 wart 5 is the live route to killing it | §4.12, CLAUDE.md §11 | — |
2026-08-12 | **The premise rule, turned on the compiler: nine more live defects, and twenty premises that turn out to be five claims.** The worst is a **NaN as a map key** — `{f64: int}` is legal and `0.0 / 0.0` is legal, the probe rests on the key's `eq` being reflexive, and nothing said so. Inserted twice, `len` was 2; the lookup answered `missing_key`; `{nan: 1} == {nan: 1}` was false. **Exit 0, ASan clean, leak counter balanced — every instrument this project owns reported success**, which is the class §1.2 exists to eliminate, and the project had already ruled on the identical hazard for `sort`. The second is the lexer walking to `text.len()` under a doc comment saying *"one source file"*: since M8a the indent stack and the bracket list crossed the file boundary, so an unclosed `(` in `main.hero` swallowed all of `geom.hero` **and** the commonest state a file is ever in — a half-written `y =` — answered `internal error … exit 2`. The third: a user file named `library.hero` had its declarations resolvable unqualified everywhere, because four sites identify the library by module *name* while the collision check skips a pair whose modules are *equal* — a hole exactly at identity | the roots are the finding, not the count: **"the runtime may trust its caller"** carries five of them, **"one byte is one column"** three, and a premise depended on in three places is one test rather than three. `sort`'s NaN abort and the map's missing one were the same rule at two sites, decided once and applied once | §4.9, §4.15, §4.20, §1.11, CLAUDE.md §11 | 006, 007, 026 |
2026-08-12 | **The premise audit closed: twenty premises, five roots, seventeen more repairs — and one instrument that fired on its first day.** The roots are the record, not the count. *"One byte is one column"* had **three** faces (the formatter's 88-column rule, the caret's width, and the reported column itself), and the doc defending byte columns cited `#line`, which carries a file and a line and **never a column** — the premise defending the premise was dead too. *"The runtime may trust its caller"* had four, of which `hero_str_from_bytes` validating nothing is the one due to die at M7: `slice`, `chars` and `len` all rest on well-formed UTF-8 and nothing checked it, held up only by a gate that refuses `extern` today. *"Counting as a proxy for identity"* had one, and `phases.rs` had already rejected counting for the decref sweep twenty lines from the copy-out check that still counted. *"Hand-maintained lists"* had six, including `is_thesis_rule` — where a **missing** entry silently understates the number the thesis is argued from, and nothing mechanical could see it | the repair for that last one is the shape this project keeps re-deriving: pin the set of codes the golden corpus annotates to a constant, `measure::gate`'s design, so adding a diagnostic is red until somebody writes it down **and decides which side of the control arm it is on**. It fired the same afternoon, on the new `stray_carriage_return` golden — an instrument whose first act is to catch its author is the only kind worth trusting | §4.15, §4.17, §4.20, Part 11, CLAUDE.md §7, §9, §11 | 006, 011, 022 |
2026-08-12 | **Thirteen spec silences, costed as one package — and the spec got SMALLER: 2434 → 2422, net −12.** They had accumulated across eight milestones, one `Decide:` entry each, and were taken together because §1.6's budget is a single pool and thirteen separate +10s is how a document reaches its ceiling without anyone deciding it should. **Nine of the thirteen close at zero**, measured by running every draft: each wrong spelling is a loud diagnostic that prints the rule, so the tokens would buy no silence. One clause is **deleted** — *"No null. Absence is a different type (`T?`)"*, −15 — reached independently by two judges: one wrote `next: Node?` from it and terminated a list with a fabricated `fail("no_next", …)`, the other measured that `null` is already caught loudly, so the sentence was redundant *and* the misleading invitation. Four sentences land: `ok(v)` named (closure list, and its absence left `return x` in a `-> int?` function with a diagnostic carrying no note and no fix), `str` *indexed and measured in bytes* (the only genuinely silent one), the print row admitting `inf`/`-inf`/`nan`, and one word — *integer* division by zero | **the abort proposal that would have closed four silences at once was struck by three judges from three grounds, and the ffi-pragmatist compiled the counter-example in Heroes itself**: squaring a large value eleven times reaches `inf` with no division in the program, and the spec's own FFI example `extern function sqrt(x: f64) -> f64` returns NaN for negative input — so aborting makes NaN reachable *only through the door the compiler cannot check*. Zig shipped that abort and moved off it; Python traps and NumPy overrode it. The historian predicted the package would be purely additive, as every specification budget in its sample was except Oberon's, which held by removing — and this commit falsifies that prediction on purpose | §1.6, §4.6, §4.14, §4.20, spec lines 50/56/134/155 | 035, 012, 023, 030 |
2026-08-12 | **`main` may not declare a result, and the reason is that it could fail in total silence.** `function main() -> int?` returning `fail(…)` **printed nothing and exited 0** — no diagnostic, no output, and the one thing a shell can read saying the program worked. Panel 030 had reported the weaker half (a `main` that prints its error still exits 0); this is a `main` that prints none. Refused in the checker as `main_returns`, **zero spec tokens**, until M7 decides `exit(code)` — which is what a fallible `main` would have to mean | five languages, one direction, none back: C99 fixed falling off `main`'s end, Haskell treats any other failure as `exitFailure`, Rust shipped `?`-in-`main` at 1.26, Python exits 1 on an uncaught exception — and **Node.js is this exact situation**, warning and exiting 0 from 2016 until Node 15 made it fatal in 2020, four years that ended when CI could not see the error | §4.2, ROADMAP M18 | 035, 030 |
2026-08-12 | **Four live defects found beside the questions rather than in them.** `%` on two `f64` reached clang as C's `%` on two `double`s — **exit 2 on a program spec line 139 explicitly allows** — now `fmod`, which truncates toward zero exactly as line 135 already requires, so the C function and the sentence agree without either changing. A `match` as the inline body of another `match`'s arm, **open since M4**, was one accidental production: after the inner match consumed its `Dedent` the postfix loop read the next arm's leading `.` as a field, so the error landed on a line nobody had written wrong — five lines, and the port has 45 sites that want it. The build cache carried `heroes::VERSION`, which does not move when the emitter does, so **rebuilding the compiler reused the previous one's artifacts** — two debugging detours in one milestone were stale output read as real. And `hero_str_from_bytes`'s new UTF-8 validation aborted on `getenv` of a Latin-1 value, on a PNG, and on a SQLite column another program wrote: `hero_utf8_valid` is exported so a binding can branch instead of dying | the last is the sharpest, because it was **this morning's own fix**: validation was right and its *shape* was wrong — an abort on bytes the environment chose rather than bytes the program chose, which is a different thing from `to_int` aborting on a value the program computed | §4.14, §4.19, §4.20, §3.1 | 035 |
2026-08-12 | **Milestone identifiers become names, and the algorithm that assigns them is CLAUDE.md §14.** `M8a` is `M-module-namespace`, `M7` is `M-ffi-ladder`, `M8c` is `M-selfhost-fixpoint`; two words, naming the deliverable and never the area, so the area stays free for the second milestone that touches it — `M-module-namespace` and `M-separate-compilation` are both about modules, and the trap had already closed once. **The record is not rewritten**: `docs/panel/`, this file, `docs/journal/`, `docs/measurements/`, `docs/defects/`, `docs/book/beats.md`, `tests/golden/`, every commit subject and the twelve tags `m0`–`m6` and `m8a` keep the numbers they were written with, and `docs/ROADMAP.md` § The names resolves them. A numbered RFC/PEP-style series (`HEP-001`) — the scheme R7 cited approvingly — was evaluated and **refused**: the event that broke this project's numbering was a **reorder**, not an insertion (panel 030 moved M8a ahead of M7), and gapped numbering covers insertions only, while nine of sixteen rows carry `scheduled, no warrant` and may be reordered again | panel 030 R7 refused *renumbering*, where the old id still resolves to the wrong thing silently; a rename into a disjoint namespace fails **loudly** and cannot be handed to a different milestone later. R7's condition — the explicit execution-order table — survives; what goes is the nine-line paragraph above it, because a name makes no claim about position, retiring R7's own objection that this is "a rule a reader has to be told twice" | §14, Part 10 | 030 (amended) |
2026-08-12 | **The FFI emits `<header.h>` and never `"header.h"`, and every `extern` carries a `_Generic` `_Static_assert` on its return type.** Compiled, not argued: with a decoy `sqlite3.h` beside the generated unit, the quoted form read the decoy with **zero diagnostics under `-Weverything`** — panel 020's decoy-`runtime/` finding relocated to a header, where the `_Static_assert(HERO_RUNTIME_ABI)` that rescued it cannot be written. The assertion's controlling expression is a call C11 6.5.1.1p3 does not evaluate but does type-check, so it costs one line per `extern` and nothing at runtime — 11 of 11 correct ladder bindings pass, `size_t` turns loud | a mechanism whose guarantee is false for the pair its own second rung uses is worse than no mechanism, because the reader trusts it | §4.19, §3.1 | 036 |
2026-08-12 | **File I/O, `args()` and `exit(code)` are named by the spec and delivered as Tier 2 over a `hero_os.h`, not as built-ins.** Three judges against one: a `T?` is a per-translation-unit generated struct the C runtime cannot name, so `read_file -> str?` needs a second `print`-shaped emitter arm and an ABI bump (§1.7's mechanical test); Pascal predeclared all three of these and Kernighan's 1981 paper itemises all three as defects, and Oberon predefines none of them. The one vote for built-ins was the reader's and it decided the *spec* half: file I/O through the FFI as specified is impossible rather than awkward — no buffer type, no allocation, no `cstr`→`str` — so the four names and signatures are in the prompt and the route is not | the spec says what a reader must know; where the code lives is not something a reader can observe, and Odin's `core:os` redesign was possible only because it was a package | §1.11, §4.20, §1.7 | 036 |
2026-08-12 | **`ptr` gets exactly one literal, `null`, and a C out-parameter is an `@` parameter.** The llm-ergonomist could not write the milestone's own acceptance program: `sqlite3_open(path, &db)` needs a `ptr` value and the language produces none — no null, no address-of, no allocation, and every binding is initialised. 2026-08-12 | **The reserved bitwise set moves from a spec sentence to a diagnostic, and `<<`/`>>` are lexed rather than left to the parser.** `6 & 3` used to say `` `&` is not part of the language's syntax (ASCII-only) `` — false, since `&` *is* ASCII, and the parenthetical sent the reader hunting for an encoding fault — while `1 << 2` said "expected an expression, found `<`", which names neither the set nor the reason. 2026-08-12 | **`not_fallible` carries a `certain` fix, so `--apply` can do what the message asks.** The message said "remove the `?`" while carrying no `Fix`, which is the same edit asked for twice; the fix deletes the span between the operand's end and the expression's, both extents taken from the expression in hand rather than from the `?` being the last character. Pays for the spec's deleted sentence (−15) | CLAUDE.md §8 makes a machine-applicable fix the difference between a diagnostic and a round-trip; the spec line was affordable only once the compiler could close the loop | §4.6, §4.17, CLAUDE.md §8 | 036 |
2026-08-12 | **The return-type assertion accepts every signed integer plus every unsigned one narrower than 64 bits, and the controlling expression is `+(call)`.** Found by binding libcurl (author instruction, ladder rung 4): `CURLcode` is an `enum`, `_Generic` selects on an enum's own type rather than on `int`, and the first version of the macro therefore refused **every enum-returning C function in existence** — most of libcurl, OpenSSL and raylib. Invisible against SQLite, which returns plain `int`. Unary `+` applies the integer promotions; `CURLcode` promotes to `unsigned int`, which fits `int64_t` without losing a value, while `size_t` stays unsigned 64-bit and keeps firing | a mechanism verified against one library is verified against one library, and the second rung of the ladder found in one compile what no reading of the C standard had | §4.19, §4.3 | 036 |
2026-08-12 | **`to_str` gains a `cstr` row, and it is the FFI boundary's return path.** `.cstr()` lends a `str` to C; `to_str(c)` copies one back through `hero_str_from_cstr`. §4.20 says those primitives exist for exactly this — *"without them no `extern function` may return `str` and §4.19's ladder is unwritable at step 3, read a result, because every C library returns strings as borrowed pointers"* — and panel 036's ffi-pragmatist predicted the need before the ladder reached a library that returns one. Panel 036's spec-warden predicted the failure before the signature existed | a type the language can write but no expression can produce is a hole that only appears when something needs it, which is why it survived six milestones | §4.6, §4.3 | 036 |

2026-08-12 | **In-place `push` at refcount 1 is refused: it fires nowhere, and where it fires it corrupts.** Implemented verbatim by two judges independently. The gate never opens — `own.rs` rule 5 gives a call's result a synthetic owning slot while the named slot increfs, so every accumulator push sees refcount **2**: 50 000 of 50 000, and the curve is unchanged (9.27 s vs 8.33 s). Where it does open it is wrong: an array held only by an enclosing array's element slot has refcount 1 and a live observer, so `grid[0]` grows behind `grid`'s back at exit 0, ASan silent, leak counter green and **68 of 68 goldens byte-identical**. The sound form is a **place store** — `p @ push(p, v)` recognised at lowering, uniqueness taken from the place rather than guessed from a count, which is the shape `hero_map_set` already has: 1 000 000 pushes in **0.004 s** against 806 s. 2026-08-12 | **CLAUDE.md §7 no longer credits the ABI stamp with catching a decoy runtime, because it does not.** A decoy that copies `HERO_RUNTIME_ABI` passes the `_Static_assert`; what protects a build against one is the **cache key**, which covers the whole runtime's contents. The stamp's real job is version skew, which the runtime search makes possible, and it is worth keeping for that | the sentence had been read as a proof for four milestones, and it was describing an intention — the same class as §4.20's single allocation point, found in the same sweep | CLAUDE.md §7 | 034, 037 |
2026-08-12 | **The queue splits into three lists, one per kind of item, and each skill is named after the list it reads.** One file held **192 open items** across three kinds — what is true, what should be true, and work with a home — and a list you have to filter before you can read it is a list nobody opens. `LEARN.md` (162) · `DECIDE.md` (20) · `SCHEDULED.md` (7); `QUEUE.md` keeps every ticked item and the index, because every commit subject cites that path (§14). The split was mechanical over the items' own text with **twenty assigned by hand**, and that map is in the commit so a misfiling is visible rather than silent — 282 items before, 282 after | a skill whose name does not match its list is one more thing to remember, and `/unblock` reading `DECIDE.md` was exactly that | CLAUDE.md §3, §14 | — |

2026-08-12 | **Metric 3's instrument stops flattering itself, and starts being attributable** (author decisions, `/decide`). The check reuses `fate`, so the verdict on a base program and the verdict on its mutants are literally the same function. **And a rate is now attributable**: `--survivors` prints each accepted mutant as its site and its two lines, `--operator` narrows to one of the twelve, and the site is derived from the two texts rather than threaded out of the edits | measurement 002 recorded two per-operator rates that moved between runs and **declined to explain either**, because attributing a delta needs the mutants behind it — CLAUDE.md §12 says measurement beats opinion, and a number nobody can attribute is an opinion with a decimal point. The flag paid for itself on its first run: `swap-args` survives mostly on `fail("code", "msg")`, two `str` arguments in a position where the language's own same-type rule would demand names if `fail` were an ordinary function | Part 11, CLAUDE.md §9, §10, §12 | 011, 038 |
2026-08-12 | **A `constant` may no longer be defined in terms of itself, and the check had to be about the cycle rather than about constants** (author decision, `/decide`; reasoning note 003 found it). `constant A: int` / `B` beside `constant B: int` / `A` used to pass every gate: `check` 0, `build` 0, and a binary that **SIGSEGVs at -O0 and hangs at -O2** — one program with two failure modes chosen by a flag nobody typed. `resolve/cycles.rs` is an iterative depth-first search over "this body reads that declaration", over **every** top-level declaration, reporting a cycle only where a `constant` with a body sits on it. `constant_cycle` is not a thesis rule and both metric-3 arms keep it: rejecting a definition that has no value is what every language does, so it belongs in the control arm too | the filter is on the cycle and not on the edges because the defect escapes: `constant A` with body `f()`, where `function f() -> int` returns `A`, was accepted for the same reason and is the same defect wearing a function. Restricting the graph to constants would have fixed the reported instance and left the class. The converse rule is what keeps it honest — a cycle of functions alone is legal, because §4.2 grants mutual recursion and a function has arguments and a base case where a constant's accessor has neither, so the golden carries `even`/`odd` and a legal `START`/`CHAIN` chain as its control. The repair at M-ffi-ladder was two changes made together — the `+`, and widening the accepted set to `unsigned int` and its narrower siblings — and **the second alone was sufficient**, because `_Generic` on an enum selects its compatible integer type, which the widened set now contains. Econ.* 68:85-123, 1963-2022 panel); FDAAA 801's 12-month deadline, fines up to $10,000/day, statute of **2007**, produced its first notice in **2021**, **zero** penalties ever, and 41% compliance (DeVito et al., *Lancet* 2020). 2026-08-14 | **Robustness is a goal of the language, and it wins** (author instruction: *"uno dei motivi per cui lo voglio fare è che produca codice che non abbia problemi di segmentation fault o di memoria — tutte le scelte in quella direzione vincono su altri criteri"*). A Heroes program must not segfault and must not corrupt memory. Where a choice runs toward that, it beats every other criterion in CLAUDE.md: elegance, token cost, ergonomics, the size of the compiler, and speed. Written into §12 because it is a **precedence** rule and not a new permission — it does not suspend Principle 0, which still binds what *enters*; it decides the case where two admissible forms disagree and one of them can be made to crash. It is the mirror of §13's performance line rather than an exception to it: performance is not a goal *and* not a licence, safety is a goal *and* a tie-break. The sitting was convened on a verified gap: `v == nullptr` on a `cstr` was `expected cstr, found ptr`, and declaring the binding `-> ptr` bought the comparison at the cost of ever reading the string — **you had to choose between checking for null and reading it**. Four options were proposed and **the sitting's own premise was false**: `to_str` on a null `cstr` never dereferenced anything, because `runtime/parts/str.c` guards it and panics with its own name, identical at `-O0`, `-O2` and `--sanitize`.
